# Text to Lattice security model

Typing stays local until confirmation. hah.dev then sends the passage through Hugging Face and OpenRouter to the named providers. Admission limits constrain work, the application avoids storing passage content, and a hard reload clears the visitor’s text.

[Consent and destinations](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/text-to-lattice-security-model.html#sec-01) · [Resource bounds](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/text-to-lattice-security-model.html#sec-04) · [Content retention](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/text-to-lattice-security-model.html#sec-07)

[Consent and admission bound each submission](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/evidence.html#admission-and-consent) · [Safari and Brave completed the visitor path](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/evidence.html#browser-results)

[Reading guide (JSON)](https://hah.dev/documentation/text-to-lattice/reading-guide.json)

<details>
<summary>Full technical reference</summary>


This is an as-built source model, not a penetration test, formal semantic proof, privacy guarantee, or deployed-control attestation.

## Authority and claim boundary

This register describes the Lattice method at an architectural level and the bounded Text to Lattice implementation evidenced in this repository. It does not redistribute the exclusive register profile, certify universal conformance, or convert source tests into deployed proof.

Stable identifiers persist. A claim changes here with cited evidence; a rendered filter, expansion, or downloaded view never changes authoritative state.

The technical reference is generated from the atlas. The editorial overview and cited examples come from app/content/projectDocuments.js.

## Scope and decision rule

The fixed Hugging Face router sends requests through OpenRouter to the DeepInfra-qualified Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets. This release selects Qwen/Qwen3-235B-A22B-Instruct-2507:deepinfra and meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct:coreweave. Parasail and Venice remain manual backup choices. Each request uses one selected route with no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, DeepInfra and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. DeepInfra describes deletion of ordinary inputs and outputs after inference. Its terms allow temporary in-memory caching, operational metadata, and legal or elected-feature exceptions. With written authorization, DeepInfra may retain data to resolve a support issue. It deletes that data within 30 days after resolving the issue. OpenRouter can retain metadata, categorize prompts and retain content for safety or legal reasons. Its settings for the Hugging Face route are unknown. CoreWeave describes zero retention by default. Tracing settings for this route are unknown. Previous Venice entry follows verbatim as dated history. The manually selected Venice third backup for the same Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets uses the fixed Hugging Face router through OpenRouter. DeepInfra remains the standard generator service. Parasail remains the previous manually selected backup. The selected generator is Qwen/Qwen3-235B-A22B-Instruct-2507:venice. Review and certification remain meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct:coreweave. The selection is made in reviewed source. Each request uses only that route with no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, Venice and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. Venice and potentially a compute partner handle generation. Venice describes different privacy tiers. The tier and compute partner for this Hugging Face route are unverified. Venice’s terms allow content monitoring under its privacy policy. OpenRouter metadata, prompt categorization and applicable safety or legal retention remain disclosed. Hugging Face route settings are unknown. CoreWeave’s default serverless zero-retention statement does not establish this route’s tracing setting. External-provider retention is not guaranteed by hah.dev. Previous Parasail entry follows verbatim as dated history. The manually selected Parasail backup for the same Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets uses the fixed Hugging Face router through OpenRouter. DeepInfra remains the standard generator service. The selected generator is Qwen/Qwen3-235B-A22B-Instruct-2507:parasail; review and certification remain meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct:coreweave. The selection is made in reviewed source. Each request uses only that route with no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, Parasail and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. Parasail’s serverless service retains inputs and outputs through generation and delivery. It may inspect masked usage metadata for service or support. It does not train on visitor content without explicit agreement. OpenRouter metadata, prompt categorization and applicable safety or legal retention remain disclosed. Hugging Face route settings are unknown. CoreWeave’s default serverless zero-retention statement does not establish this route’s tracing setting. External-provider retention is not guaranteed by hah.dev. Previous standard-generation entry follows verbatim as dated history. During the owner-authorized public G06 qualification, the service admits requests without either daily admission cap or an automatic service expiry. The operator restores the held API before the held page on completion or failure. Ordinary deployment enforces 30 transformations globally and 3 per browser cookie jar each UTC day. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, DeepInfra and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. DeepInfra serves the fixed Qwen3-235B-A22B-Instruct-2507 generator. CoreWeave serves the Llama 3.3 70B Instruct verifier and certifier. Hugging Face routes both models through OpenRouter. OpenRouter metadata, prompt categorization and applicable safety or legal retention remain disclosed. The Hugging Face route settings are unknown. DeepInfra describes ordinary deletion with temporary caching, support, legal and feature exceptions. CoreWeave describes zero data retention by default for serverless inference. Optional tracing can record requests. The tracing setting for this route is unknown. External-provider retention, deletion and processing behavior are not guaranteed by hah.dev. Previous implementation entry follows verbatim as dated history. Hugging Face, OpenRouter and DeepInfra process user text after confirmation. Hugging Face routes both fixed models through OpenRouter. DeepInfra serves Qwen for generation and Llama 3.3 for verification and certification. The following entry records the earlier configuration. Current owner-directed API phase telemetry, 2026-11-02: the canonical client opts into application/vnd.hah.lattice.events.v1+ndjson on its existing single content-bearing POST, after the unchanged bodyless visitor-session setup. Default JSON callers and the existing JSON qualification canary remain unchanged. Only the 13 closed phase names from actual engine progress are transmitted before the existing strictly validated terminal result or error; progress carries no source, candidate, result, identifier, count, percentage or remaining-time estimate. Contiguous sequence numbers, at most 128 phase frames plus one terminal, 262144 total UTF-8 bytes including framing and a 512-byte error reserve remain finite. The client returns a result only after one valid terminal and EOF; stream HTTP 200 alone is not transformation success. Admission precedes stream allocation. The original 240000-millisecond deadline and qualification expiry govern producer completion; cancellation aborts the pipeline, the browser Web Lock lasts through terminal/EOF or bounded abort, and phase failure cannot become success. No additional request, provider opportunity, automatic retry, polling, reconnect, fallback or persistence is introduced. The exact v22 diagnostic request header combined with stream Accept is rejected before admission; default JSON diagnostics remain unchanged. Fresh backend and native evidence must qualify the actual current provider and streaming client; no source edit or local test closes GATE-02, GATE-03 or GATE-06. The complete prior value follows verbatim as dated history. Owner-selected OVHcloud review-role migration, 2026-11-02: the fixed Hugging Face router uses DeepInfra-qualified Qwen generator and OVHcloud-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets. Both current review stages retain strict json_schema and the existing stopped single assistant nonempty string-content profile with optional empty tool_calls. Both Scaleway-only optional-null flags are false for OVHcloud; the independent legacy Scaleway selector/router guard remains unchanged and is not retargeted. The five optional null message metadata fields are rejected for OVHcloud. Prompts, fitted schemas, wire/host/semantic checks, output and byte ceilings, deadlines, calls, quotas, privacy, no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback remain unchanged. Exact OVHcloud envelope, full fitted-schema support, managed tokenization/input fit, successful billing and behavior remain unproved and may cause qualification to refuse.  The complete prior value follows verbatim as dated history. U28/D30 current qualification scope: fixed DeepInfra Qwen generation and Scaleway Llama 3.3 verification/certification through the same-origin API and fixed Hugging Face router; integration and exact-target qualification remain pending. Earlier Nscale role wording below is retained for historical scope. Threat model for the active hah.dev Text to Lattice remote capability: deliberate browser submission, content-free visitor-session setup and one content request under the same origin-wide Web Lock, same-origin API validation, fixed server-side Hugging Face, DeepInfra and Nscale processing, bounded response handling, and application-level nonretention. Historical WebLLM, Turnstile, and lease controls are inactive and cannot satisfy this model.

Prioritize controls where consequence, realistic plausibility, and lifecycle reuse are jointly high. Preserve uncomfortable residuals: probabilistic semantic drift, external-provider processing and retention, public-endpoint abuse, output capture, supply-chain change, and the gap between source checks and production evidence.

The decision boundary is:

$$\text{marginal value} \propto \text{consequence} \times \text{plausibility} \times \text{lifecycle value}$$

An unusual finding remains eligible. Novelty alone neither promotes nor dismisses it.

| Classification | Test | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| High marginal value | Realistic failure, meaningful consequence, or reusable architectural benefit | Fix now |
| Moderate marginal value | Lower probability but cheap and bounded to fix or test | Fix when bounded |
| Low marginal value | Exotic, nonconsequential, or provider-impossible with little lifecycle value | Document or defer |
| Negative marginal value | Bespoke complexity or new failure surface exceeds risk removed | Do not implement |

## Protected assets

| ID | Asset | Objective |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ASSET-01 | Visitor source, prompts, candidates, and results | Confidentiality, integrity, exact transmission authorization, and no application persistence. |
| ASSET-02 | Semantic obligations and outcome labels | Coverage, polarity, modality, quantity, uncertainty, ordering, accessibility, and honest withholding. |
| ASSET-03 | Named browser capability and provider credential | Exact same-origin request construction and server-only HF_TOKEN secrecy. |
| ASSET-04 | Provider budget and service availability | Bounded request, provider-call, completion, response-size, and retry behavior under public traffic. |
| ASSET-05 | Endpoint, provider, model, schema, and prompt integrity | Fixed routing and roles without user-selected targets, silent substitution, or fallback. |
| ASSET-06 | Public disclosures and release evidence | Traceability to exact source and deployed behavior without overstating privacy, accessibility, anti-abuse, or conformance. |

| TB-04 | Bounded request memory and persistence surfaces | Source, prompts, candidates, results, and raw provider bodies may exist transiently in request memory but have no application database, object store, cache, queue, raw-content log, or analytics sink. |
| TB-05 | Provider response and public result | Closed structured output, deterministic fail-closed host validation, size ceilings, and text-only rendering separate untrusted provider output from public outcome claims. |
| TB-06 | Source register and generated documentation | Escaped deterministic projections expose current claims and clearly marked historical evidence but no visitor content, secrets, or private profile inventory. |

## Threat register

| ID | Threat | Marginal value | As-built posture | Pre-publication work | Residual boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SEC-01 | Content is transmitted without valid authorization or to an undeclared destination | High | The browser capability is fixed to same-origin POST /api/lattice. Under one origin-wide Web Lock it sends a content-free visitor-session setup followed by exactly one content request containing text, requested_mode, and schema_version 1. Open, edit, validation failure, pre-submit dismissal, and cancellation create no setup or transformation request; a transport or non-204 setup failure prevents the content request; if an accepted setup's HttpOnly cookie is blocked or dropped, the Worker rejects the content request before admission or provider work; terminal failures do not automatically retry. Diagnostic revision 9 closes the missing HTTP-header observation exposed by run #176; it does not claim to repair the upstream HTTP 405. Only a marked, unexpired qualification request enables five synchronous, bounded header classifications on an already rejected HTTP response. No error body is read. The finite observations describe advertised Content-Type, Allow, router-route presence, and literal model/provider comparisons; error origin and actual serving identity remain unestablished. Every header is limited to 256 characters and Allow to 32 list members, with case-sensitive POST matching and lawful empty-list handling. Missing, malformed, oversized, and throwing access produce fixed categories. The producer requires exactly five frozen own data properties; the production checker requires this complete group only on provider_http_error and rejects partial, unknown, success, or incompatible groups. HTTP status and failure-class provenance must agree. Ordinary API JSON, unread-body cancellation, provider request bytes, selectors, verifier/certifier 2048/520 ceilings, deadlines, retries, admission, aggregate exposure, privacy boundaries, rights, and held public state remain unchanged. The earlier revision-8 observations retain their original meanings and provenance. This candidate requires fresh local/CI gates and one newly frozen bounded qualification, followed by evaluated results and verified held restoration; GATE-02/03/06 remain open. Word, code-unit, request-byte, passage, work-group, completion, provider-output, response-size, and repair ceilings are explicit. The content-free setup makes no quota claim or provider call, and missing-cookie content fails 428 before both. After exact content validation, one atomic claim admits at most 30 transformations globally per UTC day and 3 per cooperating canonical client with an ordinary persistent browser cookie jar per UTC day; quota identity and state use no IP address, browser fingerprint, submitted content, or result. These daily admission limits apply in standard deployment. The operator-ended browser qualification lifts both. Every admitted attempt counts after disconnect or provider failure without refund, and its pipeline receives no second shared quota claim. At most 32 provider calls per admitted request bounds admitted daily provider exposure at 960 calls. The browser and Worker use authoritative 240-second whole-request deadlines; analysis, candidate, certification, and repair calls have 120-second ceilings and verification has a 180-second ceiling, while the whole-request deadline may end any stage sooner. The separate 30-per-60-second path limiter shapes edge bursts. Verification remains capped at 2,048 output tokens against an exhaustively measured 1,326-token worst supported complete legal output, leaving 722 tokens or 54.45 percent; certification remains capped at 520 against a conservative accepted legal maximum of 440, leaving 80. Under ordinary daily admission limits, maximum requested-output exposure is 2,048 per call, 65,536 per admitted request, 196,608 per cooperating browser cookie jar per UTC day, and 1,966,080 globally per UTC day. A length finish fails closed without prefix parsing or automatic retry. Qualification run #158 attempt 2 exercised the predecessor private representation under the 2,048-token verification ceiling and observed a bounded length finish at verification call ordinal 3. Because no raw provider body or result was retained, that record does not establish prior-call outcomes, the size or validity of a discarded object, or provider-managed token accounting; the private-representation correction was subsequently merged by PR #50 and deployed by run #160 for bounded qualification, but remains unqualified. Run #162 subsequently deployed diagnostic revision 5 and observed repeated response-shape host validation at verification; its aggregate call count establishes neither a failure ordinal nor the exact invalid private structure or rejection origin. The next diagnostic refinement remains source-only and makes no capacity or qualification claim. Rate-limited and terminal failures expose bounded machine errors and optional bounded Retry-After without automatic retry. Each request uses one selected route with no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, DeepInfra and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. DeepInfra describes deletion of ordinary inputs and outputs after inference. Its terms allow temporary in-memory caching, operational metadata, and legal or elected-feature exceptions. With written authorization, DeepInfra may retain data to resolve a support issue. It deletes that data within 30 days after resolving the issue. OpenRouter can retain metadata, categorize prompts and retain content for safety or legal reasons. Its settings for the Hugging Face route are unknown. CoreWeave describes zero retention by default. A failed verifier correction may additionally expose only the complete finite prior verification rejection boundary/category/rule triplet retained by the engine against that exact error object. Registered value and stage/attempt/call consistency checks prevent copied, spoofed or incompatible evidence from becoming an observation. The triplet does not contain text, provider payloads or exception messages and cannot establish verification success. Ordinary and expired responses omit these diagnostics; the canary rejects partial or inconsistent groups. No new content retention or logging is introduced. Current v12 qualification diagnostics refine only an already-rejected passage failed-check width into V14LE (empty), V14LS (nonempty short) or V14LG (long), all wire-decoder/value-domain. Legacy V14L remains the undifferentiated width predicate for retained evidence. Existing identity-bound tuples carry these finite observations without raw masks, exact nonzero lengths, new fields or new headers. All variants retain the existing mask-width correction request and rejection behavior. No positive verification, new repair eligibility or release claim follows from a width observation. The API configuration defines no application database, object storage, cache, or queue for content. The Worker and adapter do not emit raw-content logs or analytics and return Cache-Control: no-store. The browser excludes /api/lattice from service-worker or cache interception under the named capability contract. | SEC-08 | Automated or distributed traffic exhausts provider capacity | High | Input, time, completion, and response ceilings cap per-request work. The canonical client serializes setup and content under a Web Lock; content requires a valid cookie; the Durable Object exact global 30-per-day counter remains authoritative. Rate-limited responses are closed, bounded, and require a new deliberate browser submission. Static semantic documentation does not depend on provider availability. | Document and probe deployed rate and concurrency controls, exact global admission, scoped cooperating-cookie-jar behavior, and provider budget alarms. Do not claim anti-bot, per-person identity, resistance to deliberate cookie rotation, or guaranteed availability without corresponding deployed evidence. | Intentional cookie clearing and uncooperative callers can rotate the per-browser identity; distributed callers can still consume the finite exact global capacity. Origin enforcement and per-location shaping do not establish human use or a pre-Worker global invocation ceiling. |
| SEC-09 | Generated output is captured despite interface friction | Moderate | Selection, copy, context menu, print, drag, and common save gestures are deterred on the result surface. The UI and public documentation say that browser controls cannot prevent determined capture. | Keep the residual visible and retest keyboard and assistive-technology access after interaction changes. Do not add extraction instructions to the interface or claim capture prevention. | Screenshots, cameras, privileged extensions, developer tools, and memory access remain possible. A visitor can always manually reproduce visible content. |
| SEC-10 | Source, deployment, register, generated documentation, or public copy drifts | High | The atlas is the documentation source of truth and generated files carry deterministic digests. The release register projects exact gate records and the verifier fails closed on source, artifact, route, and deployment mismatch. Historical browser-local evidence is retained under explicitly inactive records and cannot satisfy the remote gates. | Regenerate documentation only after the release register contains the exact current remote gate projection. Qualify and deploy the exact source revision, then capture remote production evidence before activation. | Repository checks do not prove which bytes or secrets are deployed. A provider can change behavior without a repository diff. |

## Detailed treatment records

<details id="sec-01">
<summary><strong>SEC-01</strong> · Content is transmitted without valid authorization or to an undeclared destination — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Open, edit, validation, prefetch, cancellation, implicit submit, setup failure, blocked cookie storage, automatic retry, a user-controlled target, or a client-visible provider call sends content before deliberate confirmation or outside the named path.
- **Consequence:** Sensitive text leaves the browser contrary to the visitor's choice and the public disclosure.
- **Plausibility:** Ordinary UI, transport, and configuration regressions can create premature, duplicate, or misdirected requests.
- **Lifecycle value:** One named capability and one explicit-action boundary remain reusable across interface and provider revisions.
- **Classification:** Unauthorized or misdirected disclosure is high consequence, integration-plausible, and cheaply prevented by exact request construction and deployment probes.
- **Assets:** ASSET-01, ASSET-03, ASSET-05, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-01, TB-02, TB-03
- **As built:**
  - The browser capability is fixed to same-origin POST /api/lattice. Under one origin-wide Web Lock it sends a content-free visitor-session setup followed by exactly one content request containing text, requested_mode, and schema_version 1.
  - Open, edit, validation failure, pre-submit dismissal, and cancellation create no setup or transformation request; a transport or non-204 setup failure prevents the content request; if an accepted setup's HttpOnly cookie is blocked or dropped, the Worker rejects the content request before admission or provider work; terminal failures do not automatically retry.
  - The fixed Hugging Face router sends requests through OpenRouter to the DeepInfra-qualified Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets. <details id="sec-02">
<summary><strong>SEC-02</strong> · Hostile prose or provider output acquires authority or execution — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Prompt injection, control characters, bidi abuse, HTML-like content, or provider-authored issue text becomes an instruction, executable markup, semantic authority, or generated-document markup.
- **Consequence:** The pipeline can ignore its contract, misrepresent a finding, execute injected content, or expose unsafe markup.
- **Plausibility:** Directly plausible because arbitrary prose and probabilistic provider output are intended inputs.
- **Lifecycle value:** Inert serialization, closed schemas, host-authored public fields, and text-only rendering protect future model and document revisions.
- **Classification:** The input and provider boundaries are necessarily hostile, so containment is a primary release condition.

<details id="sec-03">
<summary><strong>SEC-03</strong> · Semantic drift is released as success — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Generation drops, invents, negates, broadens, narrows, or reorders a material obligation and a correlated checker accepts the candidate.
- **Consequence:** Safety instructions, conditions, quantities, uncertainty, causality, consequences, or recovery relationships change while the interface claims success.
- **Plausibility:** Probabilistic server-side generation makes drift realistic even with structured output.
- **Lifecycle value:** A distinct verifier, deterministic sentinels, source-grounded reanalysis, and honest withholding remain useful across provider revisions.
- **Classification:** Meaning preservation is the product's central consequence; incorrect success is worse than refusal.
- **Assets:** ASSET-01, ASSET-02
- **Boundaries:** TB-01, TB-03, TB-05
- **As built:**
  - Protected spans and semantic sentinels preserve explicit high-consequence content.
  - Fixed Qwen generation is checked by fixed Llama verification, deterministic validators, source-grounded reanalysis, bounded repair, and whole-document certification. An identity-owned D14 finding for the same passage under a rewrite plan sets normalized materiality to false before verifier issue and decision consistency. The host supplies a binding negative from its existing deterministic check, never positive semantic acceptance. This negative is carried into the existing bounded repair feedback. Unowned, cloned, other-passage and retained-plan findings cannot supply it; unsupported contradictions without D14 still fail closed. A copied repair remains withheld, and a material repair still requires independent reverification and applicable certification. A restored D14 candidate may consequently receive the existing semantic-rejection held diagnostic category; that classification supplies no public success claim. A separate bounded recovery may repair an authentic D14 nonmaterial rewrite after unavailable initial verification only when the identity-owned terminal record is attempt 2 and both response-shape failures are wire-decoder/coverage V16M or V17M, with D14-only findings bound to current rewrite passages. The repair receives verification null rather than unavailable placeholder checks; it must earn fresh deterministic and full independent verification plus applicable certification. This uses the existing repair opportunity, never supplies missing support or positive semantic acceptance, and cannot be enabled by copied diagnostics, other failures, or mixed findings. Current v12 qualification diagnostics refine only an already-rejected passage failed-check width into V14LE (empty), V14LS (nonempty short) or V14LG (long), all wire-decoder/value-domain. Legacy V14L remains the undifferentiated width predicate for retained evidence. Existing identity-bound tuples carry these finite observations without raw masks, exact nonzero lengths, new fields or new headers. All variants retain the existing mask-width correction request and rejection behavior. No positive verification, new repair eligibility or release claim follows from a width observation.
  - Five outcome states prevent withheld or unresolved work from being relabeled as complete; remote clarification is disabled.
- **Required before publication:**
  - Run the full adversarial and semantic regression corpus against the exact remote release revisions.
  - Record manual review across realistic genres without treating samples as universal proof.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - No bounded corpus or fixed model pair proves equivalence for all prose.
  - Review-required is semantically releasable but explicitly not complete bounded clearance.
- **Sources:** SRC-PROTECTED, SRC-VALIDATORS, SRC-DEMO, SRC-HF-ADAPTER, SRC-TEST-ENGINE, SRC-TEST-API

</details>

<details id="sec-04">
<summary><strong>SEC-04</strong> · Input or remote inference exhausts bounded resources — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Pathological Unicode, oversized JSON, dense passages, excessive completions, stalled provider calls, repair loops, oversized responses, repeated ordinary-browser use, or distributed valid requests consume bounded browser, Worker, provider, or account resources.
- **Consequence:** The interaction hangs, legitimate visitors exhaust the exact daily admission, provider spend exceeds the intended demonstration budget, or a response exceeds safe client handling.
- **Plausibility:** Realistic for arbitrary public input and third-party inference.
- **Lifecycle value:** Atomic daily admission, centralized ceilings, identity minimization, and fail-closed deadlines bound future pipeline stages and provider revisions.
- **Classification:** Public arbitrary input and paid external work make resource exhaustion ordinary and consequential; an exact global cap has high value even though a cookie-jar limit cannot identify a person.
- **Assets:** ASSET-04, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-01, TB-02, TB-03, TB-04

  - Verify the 32-provider-call per-admission and 960-call maximum daily provider exposure and measure bounded provider spend and concurrency without claiming availability.
  - Deploy and qualify the current closed structured-output correction, retain only sanitized evidence, and require a complete host-validated verifier result and non-error terminal transformation before treating provider capacity as sufficient.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - Intentional cookie clearing, blocking, partitioning, or an uncooperative caller remains outside the per-browser identity claim, but the exact global accepted-transformation cap remains authoritative.
  - Distributed callers can consume the finite daily admission, and provider queues and infrastructure behavior remain outside application control.
  - Pinned local tokenizer measurements bound the supported legal output under the reviewed tokenizer but do not establish provider-managed chat-template, wrapper, reasoning, or completion accounting; bounded headroom does not guarantee a complete legal response.
- **Sources:** SRC-INPUT, SRC-REMOTE-CLIENT, SRC-API-WORKER, SRC-HF-ADAPTER, SRC-CAPACITY-POLICY, SRC-CAPACITY-CLIENT, SRC-CAPACITY-GATE, SRC-VISITOR-COOKIE, SRC-TEST-API, SRC-HF-SCALEWAY, SRC-LLAMA-3-3-LICENSE, SRC-LLAMA-3-3-AUP, SRC-D32-REQUIREMENTS, SRC-D32-EVIDENCE, SRC-HF-OVHCLOUD, SRC-OVHCLOUD-STRUCTURED, SRC-HF-CHAT-ROUTING, SRC-OPENROUTER-DATA, SRC-DEEPINFRA-TERMS, SRC-HF-MODEL-CATALOG, SRC-COREWEAVE-INFERENCE, SRC-COREWEAVE-TERMS, SRC-COREWEAVE-PRIVACY

</details>

<summary><strong>SEC-05</strong> · Endpoint, provider, model, schema, or dependency identity drifts — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Configuration, provider routing, model aliases, schemas, prompts, response transport, compatibility policy, dependencies, or terms change, or a failure silently switches provider or model.
- **Consequence:** Processing, semantics, privacy, cost, authorization, or evidence diverges from the reviewed release.
- **Plausibility:** Hosted model and dependency services can change independently of repository source even when identifiers are fixed.
- **Lifecycle value:** Exact endpoint and model allowlists plus requalification rules scale across upgrades and incidents.
- **Classification:** Silent substitution affects both semantic and data-processing claims; fixed targets and explicit requalification have high marginal value.
- **Assets:** ASSET-02, ASSET-05, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-03, TB-05, TB-06

<details id="sec-06">
<summary><strong>SEC-06</strong> · Method, path, origin, query, media type, or schema is confused — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Cross-origin callers, alternate methods or paths, query smuggling, permissive content types, confused setup/content envelopes, duplicate or extra fields, unsupported modes or versions, malformed bodies, or missing cookies reach admission or provider work.
- **Consequence:** Attackers bypass the public contract, trigger unreviewed behavior, consume budget, or cause content to be processed under false assumptions.
- **Plausibility:** Routine for a public HTTP endpoint and easy to automate.
- **Lifecycle value:** Two closed fail-closed HTTP envelopes on one named capability remain reusable regardless of provider implementation.
- **Classification:** The probes are low-cost and directly protect privacy, cost, and protocol integrity.
- **Assets:** ASSET-01, ASSET-03, ASSET-04, ASSET-05
- **Boundaries:** TB-02, TB-03
- **As built:**
  - The API accepts same-origin POST /api/lattice only as the bodyless custom-Accept visitor-session setup or the exact closed JSON content body.
  - Origin, path, query, method, body presence, content length, Accept, Content-Type, cookie, word count, mode, version, and keys fail closed before provider use; missing-cookie content returns 428 before admission or provider use.
  - Success and error envelopes are exact, bounded, and no-store.
- **Required before publication:**
  - Exercise forbidden origins, alternate paths and methods, queries, setup body or Content-Type, content missing its cookie, missing and extra fields, modes, versions, 700 and 701 words, malformed JSON, oversized bodies, and media types against deployment.
  - Confirm rejected probes and every setup request create no provider call, and setup creates no quota claim.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - Origin and signed-cookie checking are not authentication or complete anti-abuse protection.
  - Infrastructure before the Worker may still process ordinary connection metadata.
- **Sources:** SRC-REMOTE-PROTOCOL, SRC-REMOTE-CLIENT, SRC-API-WORKER, SRC-TEST-CAPABILITY, SRC-TEST-API

</details>

<details id="sec-07">
<summary><strong>SEC-07</strong> · Application storage, logging, caching, queues, or analytics retain content — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** A database binding, object store, cache, service worker, raw request or provider log, exception report, background queue, analytics event, or edge transformation captures content.
- **Consequence:** Source or derived text persists beyond the request and contradicts the application no-retention claim.
- **Plausibility:** Telemetry and convenience logging are ordinary service-development regressions.
- **Lifecycle value:** A sink inventory, no-store policy, and content-free observability rule protect every future endpoint revision.
- **Classification:** Persistence is high consequence and preventable within the application boundary, while external-provider behavior must remain a disclosed limitation.
- **Assets:** ASSET-01, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-02, TB-04, TB-06
- **As built:**
  - Current qualification diagnostics use the exact v12 marker and an unexpired qualification window. A failed verifier correction may additionally expose only the complete finite prior verification rejection boundary/category/rule triplet retained by the engine against that exact error object. Registered value and stage/attempt/call consistency checks prevent copied, spoofed or incompatible evidence from becoming an observation. The triplet does not contain text, provider payloads or exception messages and cannot establish verification success. Ordinary and expired responses omit these diagnostics; the canary rejects partial or inconsistent groups. No new content retention or logging is introduced. Current v12 qualification diagnostics refine only an already-rejected passage failed-check width into V14LE (empty), V14LS (nonempty short) or V14LG (long), all wire-decoder/value-domain. Legacy V14L remains the undifferentiated width predicate for retained evidence. Existing identity-bound tuples carry these finite observations without raw masks, exact nonzero lengths, new fields or new headers. All variants retain the existing mask-width correction request and rejection behavior. No positive verification, new repair eligibility or release claim follows from a width observation.
  - The API configuration defines no application database, object storage, cache, or queue for content.
  - The Worker and adapter do not emit raw-content logs or analytics and return Cache-Control: no-store.
  - The browser excludes /api/lattice from service-worker or cache interception under the named capability contract.
  - The fixed Hugging Face router sends requests through OpenRouter to the DeepInfra-qualified Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets. <details id="sec-08">
<summary><strong>SEC-08</strong> · Automated or distributed traffic exhausts provider capacity — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** One or many public callers repeatedly invoke valid requests, intentionally clear cookies, bypass the cooperating canonical client, distribute traffic, or force high-cost bounded inputs before rate controls take effect.
- **Consequence:** Legitimate visitors lose access or provider spend exceeds the intended demonstration budget.
- **Plausibility:** A public unauthenticated endpoint is discoverable and automatable.
- **Lifecycle value:** Honest availability claims, measurable quotas, provider alarms, and rapid held rollback remain valuable across capacity plans.
- **Classification:** The path is easy and consequential, but exact global admission bounds provider exposure even though the scoped per-browser identity claim cannot cover intentional cookie clearing or uncooperative callers.
- **Assets:** ASSET-04, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-02, TB-03
- **As built:**
  - Input, time, completion, and response ceilings cap per-request work.
  - The canonical client serializes setup and content under a Web Lock; content requires a valid cookie; the Durable Object exact global 30-per-day counter remains authoritative.
  - Rate-limited responses are closed, bounded, and require a new deliberate browser submission.
  - Static semantic documentation does not depend on provider availability.
- **Required before publication:**
  - Document and probe deployed rate and concurrency controls, exact global admission, scoped cooperating-cookie-jar behavior, and provider budget alarms.
  - Do not claim anti-bot, per-person identity, resistance to deliberate cookie rotation, or guaranteed availability without corresponding deployed evidence.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - Intentional cookie clearing and uncooperative callers can rotate the per-browser identity; distributed callers can still consume the finite exact global capacity.
  - Origin enforcement and per-location shaping do not establish human use or a pre-Worker global invocation ceiling.
- **Sources:** SRC-API-WORKER, SRC-HF-ADAPTER, SRC-PUBLIC-CONTRACT, SRC-TEST-API

</details>

<details id="sec-09">
<summary><strong>SEC-09</strong> · Generated output is captured despite interface friction — Moderate marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** Selection APIs, clipboard events, printing, page capture, browser extensions, developer tools, accessibility APIs, operating-system screenshots, cameras, or memory inspection obtain result text.
- **Consequence:** A visitor or third party retains output despite deterrent treatment, and an overstated claim misleads users about control.
- **Plausibility:** Browser event controls can block routine gestures but cannot control privileged or out-of-band capture.
- **Lifecycle value:** An explicit deterrence-only boundary remains truthful as browsers evolve.
- **Classification:** Ordinary extraction friction has value, while stronger prevention claims would be false and harmful to accessibility.
- **Assets:** ASSET-01, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-01, TB-05
- **As built:**
  - Selection, copy, context menu, print, drag, and common save gestures are deterred on the result surface.
  - The UI and public documentation say that browser controls cannot prevent determined capture.
- **Required before publication:**
  - Keep the residual visible and retest keyboard and assistive-technology access after interaction changes.
  - Do not add extraction instructions to the interface or claim capture prevention.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - Screenshots, cameras, privileged extensions, developer tools, and memory access remain possible.
  - A visitor can always manually reproduce visible content.
- **Sources:** SRC-OUTPUT, SRC-PUBLIC-CONTRACT, SRC-TEST-SECURITY, SRC-TEST-A11Y

</details>

<details id="sec-10">
<summary><strong>SEC-10</strong> · Source, deployment, register, generated documentation, or public copy drifts — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** A source change, deployment, secret, route, provider configuration, public disclosure, release projection, or generated artifact changes independently.
- **Consequence:** The public site makes stale privacy or capability claims, exposes a broken endpoint, or activates a revision that was not qualified.
- **Plausibility:** Multi-artifact release systems routinely drift without machine-bound projections and exact deployment evidence.
- **Lifecycle value:** Deterministic generation and exact-revision gates protect every later release.
- **Classification:** The architecture migration makes stale browser-local documentation an immediate material risk.
- **Assets:** ASSET-03, ASSET-05, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-02, TB-03, TB-06
- **As built:**
  - The atlas is the documentation source of truth and generated files carry deterministic digests.
  - The release register projects exact gate records and the verifier fails closed on source, artifact, route, and deployment mismatch.
  - Historical browser-local evidence is retained under explicitly inactive records and cannot satisfy the remote gates.
- **Required before publication:**
  - Regenerate documentation only after the release register contains the exact current remote gate projection.
  - Qualify and deploy the exact source revision, then capture remote production evidence before activation.
- **Residual boundary:**
  - Repository checks do not prove which bytes or secrets are deployed.
  - A provider can change behavior without a repository diff.
- **Sources:** SRC-RELEASE-REGISTER, SRC-RELEASE-QUALIFICATION, SRC-REQUIREMENTS, SRC-PUBLIC-CONTRACT, SRC-ROUTE-INVENTORY

</details>

<details id="sec-11">
<summary><strong>SEC-11</strong> · Prohibited or unsupported use is materially furthered — High marginal value</summary>

- **Vector:** A visitor submits unlawful, unauthorized, restricted, unsupported-language, or provider-policy-prohibited material, or the transformation materially furthers prohibited conduct.
- **Consequence:** The service or model use violates rights, license or provider terms, or materially assists harmful conduct.
- **Plausibility:** Plausible for any public prose transformation endpoint; keyword-only screening would be both overbroad and underinclusive.
- **Lifecycle value:** Purpose-and-consequence controls, visitor authorization, and provider-policy requalification remain reusable across model revisions.
- **Classification:** Consequences can be severe and the service is public, so fail-closed use boundaries have high marginal value.
- **Assets:** ASSET-02, ASSET-05, ASSET-06
- **Boundaries:** TB-01, TB-03, TB-05
- **As built:**
  - The interface requires authorization and warns against classified, controlled, privileged, export-controlled, operationally sensitive, or otherwise restricted information.
  - The prompt and result pipeline preserves purpose-and-consequence use checks and can withhold a transformation.
  - Active Llama 3.1 license and acceptable-use evidence remain separately tracked from provider serving, model provenance, and the historical Llama 3.2 MLC terms record.
  - The fixed Hugging Face router sends requests through OpenRouter to the DeepInfra-qualified Qwen generator and CoreWeave-qualified Llama verifier/certifier targets. - **Classification:** False privacy framing directly defeats meaningful authorization and is especially likely while historical local records remain auditable.

## Release qualification gates

Only `open-release-blocker` prevents activation. `satisfied-in-source` records repository evidence; `satisfied-in-production` records current observed live evidence for the permitted production gates; `historical-inactive` preserves non-authorizing evidence for a superseded architecture. Every other status preserves its distinct evidence boundary, safeguards, and follow-up.
GATE-06 records current canonical-browser evidence for one deliberately confirmed bodyless content-free same-origin POST /api/lattice setup followed after its accepted 204 under the same origin-wide Web Lock by exactly one content-bearing POST /api/lattice, a non-error remote result through the fixed Qwen generator and Llama verifier, bounded no-store handling, no automatic retry or alternate provider or model fallback, and a sanitized hah.dev application privacy trace. That evidence acknowledges the submitted body as an intentional transmission and does not claim control over Hugging Face, OpenRouter, DeepInfra, CoreWeave, or their infrastructure retention. A later route, setup, cookie, Web Lock, model, provider, response, cache, persistence, telemetry, or lifecycle drift reopens the blocker and restores held documentation-only publication.

A transport or non-204 setup failure must stop before the content request. Client script cannot observe storage of the HttpOnly cookie, so an accepted 204 whose cookie is blocked or dropped may still be followed by that one content request; the Worker must reject a missing cookie as 428 visitor_session_required before Durable Object admission or provider work, while invalid or undeclared cookies fail closed as 403 invalid_request at the same boundary. The setup must establish or preserve the signed cookie without a quota claim or provider call. ### GATE-01 · Exact release artifact

- **Rationale:** A stale or substituted artifact can bypass every reviewed control. Enforcing one reusable release workflow has high consequence reduction and lifecycle value.
- **Current evidence:** The release build verifies its source inventory and publishing artifacts before the production service check. The original browser capture and current comparison are available in the evidence appendix.
- **Evidence record:** The Pages workflow verifies the current main revision, checks the complete release fingerprint and ties publication to a successful service job.; Captured source, review and deployment receipts: https://github.com/howardhayden/folio/actions/runs/45188974550
- **Safeguards:** current-main checks fail closed at publication boundaries; the release decision is machine-checked against the exact source set; deployment actions are immutable-revision pinned
- **Follow-up:** If the source or build no longer matches its recorded identity, stop publication and restore the held service.

### GATE-02 · Remote API origin, secret, and privacy boundary

- **Rationale:** Raw visitor text leaves the browser at this boundary and a server credential authorizes external model calls. Route, schema, destination, logging, caching, and error-policy drift therefore have high privacy and cost consequences. Source controls are necessary but cannot establish the deployed route, binding, edge response, or provider behavior.
- **Current active evidence:** Backend run 501 verified the production API, its privacy boundary and the fixed provider route. The service accepts confirmed same-origin submissions. ### GATE-03 · Remote provider capacity and cost boundary

- **Rationale:** A public content-bearing endpoint can multiply model calls and cost even when application storage is absent. The exact global admission cap authoritatively bounds accepted work even when a caller clears or partitions the ordinary-browser quota cookie, while the browser limit still reduces ordinary repeated use. A missing or non-atomic production binding, quota or UTC-rollover drift, refund or in-pipeline recheck, identity expansion, provider-bound mismatch, or generic escape path would be consequential. This gate stays open until the deployed boundary is observed.

- **Safeguards:** content-free visitor-session setup and preservation make no quota claim or provider call; missing-cookie content fails 428 and invalid, tampered, or undeclared-cookie content fails 403 before admission or provider work; one atomic Durable Object claim after exact content validation and before provider work enforces 30 accepted transformations globally per UTC day and 3 per cooperating canonical client with an ordinary persistent browser cookie jar per UTC day; intentional cookie clearing and uncooperative callers remain outside the per-browser identity claim while the global counter stays exact; the API route also has a 30-per-60-second path-scoped rate-limiter binding as an edge shaper; admitted attempts count on disconnect or provider failure without refund, and their pipelines perform no second shared quota claim; quota identity and state contain no IP address, browser fingerprint, submitted content, or result; provider calls are bounded to 32 per admitted request and 960 from admitted requests per UTC day; response bytes, response characters, and total stage count are also bounded; provider timeouts and malformed responses fail closed without reflection; there is no browser retry, alternate provider fallback, model fallback, or generic proxy parameter; the public copy promises no availability and identifies provider retention as external
- **Follow-up:** Keep the public client held until the production setup/no-admission boundary, missing-cookie 428 and invalid-cookie 403 pre-admission failures, atomic content admission, edge shaper, and provider behavior are observed. Re-run the 30-global/3-cooperating-cookie-jar UTC-day admission, exact-global and scoped-per-browser claims, identity-minimization, no-refund, single-claim, 32-per-request and 960-per-day provider exposure, and cost checks after any provider, model, quota, cookie, limiter, timeout, stage-count, or response-size change. If the 2,048-token trial again ends with finish_reason=length, do not raise the output ceiling again; first retain only the sanitized initial-versus-correction attempt category and prior-validation class, then diagnose forced-tool schema and transport compliance within the existing privacy boundary.
- **Rollback condition:** Keep or return the public client to held documentation-only publication if setup consumes quota or contacts the provider; missing-cookie content fails to return 428, invalid, tampered, or undeclared-cookie content fails to return 403, or either reaches admission or provider work; atomic production admission or the edge shaper is absent or fails open; the exact global 30-per-day counter, scoped 3-per-cooperating-cookie-jar claim, no-IP/fingerprint/content identity, count-on-failure, no-refund, or single-claim semantics drift; provider work exceeds 32 calls per admitted request or 960 calls from admitted requests per UTC day; an alternate or generic proxy path appears; or observed latency, abuse, or cost behavior makes the published boundary materially false.

### GATE-04A · Llama behavior and public-use controls

- **Rationale:** The public-use controls address realistic misuse and misunderstanding. Exact provider-served model fixture execution would improve empirical confidence; a verifier false negative remains unobserved and candidate withholding depends on the verifier's classification. Source-specific confirmation, error and explicit-denial withholding, a fixed server-side response pipeline with no user-selectable tools, and mandatory human review bound the official product surface without proving classifier behavior.
- **Current evidence:** The unchanged Llama 3.3 license/AUP and ten-case source fixture are bound to the CoreWeave target. No exact CoreWeave behavioral execution or manual review is established. The existing accepted source-level residual cannot close production gates. Previous evidence follows verbatim as dated history. The active Llama 3.1 terms snapshots and attribution match the reviewed model-repository files. Source implements point-of-use disclosure, resettable source-specific confirmation, purpose-and-consequence safety, and candidate suppression, with deterministic regressions and a balanced ten-case non-operational fixture. Exact-model behavioral evidence was reset for meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct:nscale; this environment does not contain a recorded execution and manual review of that fixture against the active verifier.
- **Evidence record:** LICENSES/Llama-3.1-Community-License.txt; LICENSES/Llama-3.1-Acceptable-Use-Policy.md; docs/text-to-lattice/LLAMA-USE-EVALUATION-CASES.json; app/content/textToLatticeContent.js; app/resume/lattice/promptContract.js; tests/lattice-usage-policy.test.mjs
- **Safeguards:** an explicit safety-failing verifier result never releases a candidate; the confirmation resets when source text changes; disclosure states that model output can be unsafe or semantically wrong and requires human review
- **Follow-up:** Run and review the exact-model fixture when bounded runtime access is available; re-open the gate if observed behavior defeats candidate withholding or the published use boundary.
- **Acceptance basis:** The missing execution is a moderate empirical gap, and false-negative behavior is explicitly unobserved. The candidate uses a fixed server-side text transformation with no user-selectable tools, source-specific user confirmation, and mandatory human review; errors and explicit denials withhold output. This accepted source-level residual does not override the open deployment, capacity, or lifecycle gates.

### GATE-04B · Historical inactive Llama MLC-artifact provenance

- **Rationale:** A substituted verifier artifact would be consequential, so immutable identity and controlling terms remain mandatory. The missing publisher conversion narrative is a real but moderate custody gap after those controls, not a demonstrated incompatible or malicious artifact.
- **Historical inactive current evidence:** The verifier repository and revision are pinned and the controlling base-model terms are present; the MLC artifact repository does not provide a complete conversion and artifact-license record.
- **Historical inactive evidence record:** immutable verifier repository revision 1e80abf71e3d17cd564e2d2b63caa15cb226018e; reviewed Llama 3.2 terms and acceptable-use files; pinned tokenizer digest and WebLLM model contract
- **Safeguards:** model identifiers, revisions, and URLs plus tokenizer digests are machine-bound; runtime downloads are restricted to the pinned artifact paths; Built with Llama attribution and controlling terms remain published
- **Follow-up:** Accept publisher provenance if it becomes available or qualify a maintainable controlled conversion; re-open on any artifact, terms, or integrity drift.
- **Acceptance basis:** The exact artifact identity and base-model terms are known, but hashes do not establish the converted artifact's own authorization or notice custody; that gap remains explicitly accepted for this bounded browser-local demonstrator, which retrieves the immutable publisher-hosted artifact rather than redistributing its shards from hah.dev. Preserving pins, attribution, and requalification triggers has better lifecycle value than an indefinite block on an unavailable publisher narrative.

### GATE-04C · Historical inactive WASM provenance and license boundary

- **Rationale:** Opaque executable bytes make identity and lineage consequential. Exact SHA-256, SRI, git-blob, repository, revision, ABI, and configuration bindings materially reduce substitution and compatibility risk; pursuing theoretical reconstruction of every unavailable historical input would add substantial complexity without a plausible proportional failure reduction.
- **Historical inactive current evidence:** Exact bytes, digests, git blobs, repository revision, initial and final artifact commits, introducing pull request, and recorded TVM and MLC-LLM source revisions are recorded. The final artifact rewrite postdates the recorded TVM revision and its configuration metadata is consistent with the recorded MLC source, which supports plausibility but not provenance. The binary repository has no root license at the reviewed revision; the historical helper is workstation-specific, calls a Qwen preset absent from the recorded MLC revision, leaves Python and Cargo inputs unlocked, and has no matching independent rebuild.
- **Historical inactive evidence record:** exact WASM byte counts, SHA-256 digests, SRI values, and git blob identifiers; pinned binary repository revision and introducing pull request; recorded TVM, MLC-LLM, and tokenizer source revisions; ABI and embedded-configuration inspection
- **Safeguards:** the release workflow independently verifies byte count, SHA-256, and git blob identity; the exact two WASM and two tokenizer requests enforce their recorded SRI values; protected Cache API hits are SHA-256 checked and mismatches are evicted before use; allowed model shards remain bounded to the manifest-enumerated immutable model-revision URLs; reproducibility and artifact-level notice gaps remain explicitly unestablished
- **Follow-up:** Prefer upstream clarification or a maintainable controlled replacement when available; re-open immediately on digest, ABI, repository, runtime, or license-term drift.
- **Acceptance basis:** Exact identity and compatibility controls reduce technical substitution and incompatibility risk. They do not establish artifact-level authorization or notice custody; that separate gap remains explicitly accepted for this bounded browser-local demonstrator, which retrieves exact publisher-hosted libraries rather than redistributing them from hah.dev. A full historical reproducible rebuild is negative marginal value for this boundary because it demands bespoke reconstruction and long-term maintenance without a commensurate plausible technical-risk reduction; upstream clarification or a maintainable replacement remains eligible future work.

Safari Version 26.5 (21624.2.5.11.4), WebKit, on macOS 26.5.1 (25F80), M5 iMac, with an operator-normalized capture time of 2026-09-09T08:05:50Z, directly records the first-session sequence POST /api/text-to-lattice/lease 428, the isolated https://verify.hah.dev/turnstile/ frame and Cloudflare challenge resources, POST /api/text-to-lattice/lease 200, and DELETE /api/text-to-lattice/lease 204 with a zero-byte response. Brave 1.94.121 (Chromium 152.0.7977.83, arm64) on the same platform, with operator-normalized capture times from 2026-09-09T08:15:12Z through 2026-09-09T08:22:30Z, independently records the second-engine lease statuses 428, 200, and 204, the isolated verification origin and challenge flow, and pinned Qwen tokenizer and model-asset loading. The deployed canonical-client source correlates those ordered Brave rows to POST 428, POST 200, and DELETE 204; the screenshot itself does not independently prove the later request methods. The owner reports that a capture-wide search for the unique submitted source marker returned 0 matches across the Brave network record. That search establishes the source-specific negative check. The deployed source contract and tests keep source, clarification, candidate, verifier finding, and output browser-local and exclude them from lease, attestation, model-asset, error, or telemetry traffic; the captured request inventory shows no undocumented content-bearing route. The canonical URL, bodyless lease request and release source contracts, and exact Cloudflare official testing profile are additionally bound by the deployed source and operator record rather than inferred from screenshot pixels. Payload-pane inspection was not claimed. The UTC times are operator normalizations of the local capture filenames, not server-signed timestamps. Cloudflare automatically injected a Web Analytics tag for https://static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js into both document responses, but each CSP blocked the external beacon resource before transfer or execution (blocked:csp, 0.0 kB), and no /cdn-cgi/rum or other RUM transmission appeared. This composite evidence establishes canonical official-page integration and the observed privacy boundary under the declared testing profile; it does not establish human verification, bot resistance, token freshness or single use, hostname or action assurance, universal browser behavior, or production anti-bot assurance, and it does not establish that Cloudflare Web Analytics is disabled or that ordinary provider transport metadata is absent. Those captures remain historical evidence for the lease lifecycle and observed privacy boundary of their deployed revision; subsequent diagnosis established that the rapid DELETE followed a model-worker import failure and did not demonstrate a terminal conversion. The repair candidate adds a worker-only Vite pre-transform that preserves fourteen bare realm checks across the pinned WebLLM and web-tokenizers modules with fail-closed twelve/two count assertions, plus a final-artifact Worker-realm smoke that boots without window, completes the cached RPC, and reaches the first approved model-configuration cache request without network access. Because this changes emitted runtime behavior, GATE-06 is post-deployment verification: after deployment, repeat the supported-engine lifecycle and sanitized two-origin privacy trace and record one non-error terminal conversion. On 2026-09-11, the site owner reported that the deployed repaired runtime reached a full green progress bar after about one minute but remained at "Preparing on this device" with "Your result will appear here" and produced no terminal result. This owner report establishes the failed observable outcome; it is not a timestamped network capture, does not identify a newly verified deployment revision, and does not replace the historical evidence above. The replacement source candidate requires the exact https://hah.dev browser origin before any lease fetch, so local and Vite previews fail with a canonical-origin explanation instead of requesting a missing local API route. It adds a worker-realm capability probe before lease acquisition or model download; phase-aware WebLLM download, GPU-loading, and shader-compilation progress that becomes nondeterminate after the first completed phase; a closed worker-start acknowledgement so execution deadlines do not run while requests wait in the serialized queue; 30-second worker-start, 120-second preparation-inactivity, 720-second completion-inactivity, and 45-minute hard execution bounds; 15-second adapter and 10-second storage checks; a 30-second lease fetch-and-body deadline; a 10-second browser provenance-digest deadline; and explicit terminal errors. While the resume component remains mounted, Close, backdrop, and Escape are visibility-only in every phase and preserve input, availability or attestation request, lease-bound work, post-request processing, clarification, error, terminal state, and result for reopening; Cancel and Start over terminate or reset the current request, and component unmount tears it down; completion and error may release runtime resources, but their terminal state remains reopenable. The response-policy candidate preserves or adds Cache-Control: no-transform, and the live verifier now uses browser-shaped navigation requests and rejects conditionally injected Cloudflare beacon or RUM markers. These are source controls awaiting deployment verification; they do not establish that Cloudflare Web Analytics or RUM is disabled and do not establish a successful production conversion.

- **Follow-up:** Keep the public client held until the required deployment-bound canonical-browser records exist. Repeat the functional and privacy trace after any consequential route, setup envelope, Web Lock, schema, cookie, provider, model, response, CSP, cache, retention, telemetry, interaction, timeout, or retry-policy change.
- **Rollback condition:** Keep GATE-06 open and the public client held, or return it immediately to held documentation-only publication, if a canonical trace lacks one non-error terminal result; lacks the bodyless custom-Accept setup; emits a content request after transport or non-204 setup failure; emits more or fewer than one intended content-bearing POST /api/lattice after accepted 204; does not hold one browser Web Lock across setup and the full content response; sends a body, Content-Type, or Cookie on the first isolated setup; omits or drifts the exact value-free Set-Cookie metadata; sends an undeclared cookie or any account, authorization, or provider credential; lets setup consume quota or contact the provider; lets accepted-204 content with a blocked, dropped, or missing cookie reach admission or provider work instead of returning 428 visitor_session_required; retains a cookie value; contacts a provider from the browser; retries automatically; uses a cache or service worker; outlives a timed qualification expiry or continues beyond the operator-ended qualification; or reflects source, prompts, provider bodies, findings, or output outside the acknowledged request and bounded result.

Each request uses one selected route with no automatic retry and no alternate provider or model fallback. Hugging Face, OpenRouter, DeepInfra and CoreWeave process submitted text after confirmation. DeepInfra describes deletion of ordinary inputs and outputs after inference. Its terms allow temporary in-memory caching, operational metadata, and legal or elected-feature exceptions. With written authorization, DeepInfra may retain data to resolve a support issue. It deletes that data within 30 days after resolving the issue. - Qwen generation and Llama verification are probabilistic. Closed schemas, deterministic sentinels, independent model roles, bounded repair, and honest withholding reduce but do not eliminate semantic drift.
- Provider-managed serving is not byte-pinned or byte-equivalent to the historical MLC WebLLM artifacts. Fixed hosted identifiers do not prove immutable weights, tokenizer bytes, infrastructure, or serving implementation.
- The 3-per-day claim applies only to the cooperating canonical client with an ordinary persistent cookie jar. Intentional cookie clearing or uncooperative callers can rotate that identity, while the exact global 30-per-day admission remains authoritative; distributed traffic can still exhaust that finite capacity.
- Browser output controls deter routine copying but cannot prevent screenshots, cameras, privileged extensions, developer tools, accessibility APIs, or memory access.
- Repository source and tests do not prove deployed routes, encrypted secret bindings, edge response behavior, live-provider behavior, external retention, representative accessibility, or a successful canonical-browser lifecycle.
- Historical WebLLM, Turnstile, lease, artifact, and production-trace evidence remains useful design history but is inactive and cannot qualify the remote candidate.
